And I pushed an update without approval because waiting for another review cycle felt like it would kill the whole opportunity. Looking back, I know I should’ve stopped and talked it through.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing when you're doing something you really enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, even when it’s something I like, like coding. I’ll be working on a rendering bug and then a Slack notification pops up, or I notice some color pattern, or there’s a siren outside, and my mind just jumps to a completely different idea. Then I realize I haven’t finished the line of code I was on.\n\nDoctor: Are these difficulties you're experiencing causing problems in your relationships or at work?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s definitely causing problems. At work, my employer put me on leave after I went around the approval process and deployed changes by myself. And at home, my wife says the spending and the arguments have made things feel unstable. I know it’s been hard on both sides.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these difficulties with focus and impulsivity?\n\nPatient: It’s been about three months now. It really started around when my platform got selected for the Glendale showcase. Since then, the focus problems and impulsive decisions have been a lot more noticeable.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing these problems at home, at work, or in both settings?\n\nPatient: Both, unfortunately. At work it got serious enough that my employer put me on leave after I bypassed approval and deployed changes on my own. And at home, my wife says the spending and the arguments have made things feel unstable.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced significant symptoms of inattention or hyperactivity-impulsivity prior to the age of 12?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t remember having major problems with that as a kid, at least not before 12. Nothing that stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any problems with paying attention or sitting still in school when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Not that I can really remember. I mean, I’m sure I got distracted like any kid, but I don’t recall teachers or my parents making a big issue of me not paying attention or not sitting still. Nothing that stands out from back then.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s been getting hard to actually complete anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or have trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I feel like I’m running on this constant current. I wake up already thinking about features, layouts, press angles, code changes—just all of it—and sitting still feels like I’m wasting time or missing the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I have been doing things without really slowing down to check if they’re a good idea. I spent money we hadn’t agreed on—GPUs, print materials, even promo videos—because at the time it felt urgent. And I pushed an update without approval because waiting for another review cycle felt like it would kill the whole opportunity. Looking back, I know I should’ve stopped and talked it through.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing when you're doing something you really enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, even when it’s something I like, like coding. I’ll be working on a rendering bug and then a Slack notification pops up, or I notice some color pattern, or there’s a siren outside, and my mind just jumps to a completely different idea. Then I realize I haven’t finished the line of code I was on.\n\nDoctor: Are these difficulties you're experiencing causing problems in your relationships or at work?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s definitely causing problems. At work, my employer put me on leave after I went around the approval process and deployed changes by myself. And at home, my wife says the spending and the arguments have made things feel unstable. I know it’s been hard on both sides.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these difficulties with focus and impulsivity?\n\nPatient: It’s been about three months now. It really started around when my platform got selected for the Glendale showcase. Since then, the focus problems and impulsive decisions have been a lot more noticeable.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing these problems at home, at work, or in both settings?\n\nPatient: Both, unfortunately. At work it got serious enough that my employer put me on leave after I bypassed approval and deployed changes on my own. And at home, my wife says the spending and the arguments have made things feel unstable.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced significant symptoms of inattention or hyperactivity-impulsivity prior to the age of 12?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t remember having major problems with that as a kid, at least not before 12. Nothing that stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any problems with paying attention or sitting still in school when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Not that I can really remember. I mean, I’m sure I got distracted like any kid, but I don’t recall teachers or my parents making a big issue of me not paying attention or not sitting still. Nothing that stands out from back then.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these focus and impulsivity problems consistently across different situations and environments over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s been pretty consistent for about three months now. If my wife or coworkers slow me down with what they call “process,” I snap at them pretty hard.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period of at least two weeks where you weren't feeling impatient, irritable, or easily distracted?\n\nPatient: No, not really. This has been pretty steady for about three months now, since my platform was picked for the Glendale showcase. This feels like it started about three months ago, around when my platform got selected for the Glendale showcase. Before that, I don’t remember it being like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in your life before the past three months where you had significant problems with focus or impulsivity that lasted for more than a few months?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Before this, I can’t remember having focus or impulsivity problems that went on for months. This really started about three months ago, when my platform got selected for the Glendale showcase.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times before the past three months where you felt like you were in a similar state of high energy and decreased need for sleep, or has this been a relatively new experience for you?\n\nPatient: It’s pretty new for me. I don’t remember having times like this before. It’s really been going on for about three months, since my platform got selected for the Glendale showcase.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms of inattention and impulsivity caused you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Yes.
